\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\Pi}_{k}(q_{k})-\alpha}{q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{\widetilde{\Pi}_{k}^{0}(q_{k})-\alpha}{q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right\rvert\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\Pi}_{t}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\widetilde{\Pi}^{0}_{t}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq-\tilde{a}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}+(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})D_{k}.

Putting the latter back into (
101
) leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k+1}-q^{0}_{k+1,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k+1)}}-4(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q^{0}_{k+1,\alpha}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right\rvert-\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}(1-S_{k})

\displaystyle\leq S_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{2\gamma_{J(k)}^{2}}{\gamma_{J(k)+1}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)+1}}+\frac{2\gamma_{J(k)}}{\gamma_{J(k)+1}}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[-\tilde{a}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}+(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})D_{k}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}}\right)

\displaystyle=S_{k}\frac{\gamma_{J(k)}}{\gamma_{J(k)+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2\gamma_{J(k)}+(1-2\tilde{a}\gamma_{J(k)})\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}+2(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq S_{k}\frac{\gamma_{J(k)}}{\gamma_{J(k)+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2\gamma_{J(k)}+(1-2\tilde{a}\gamma_{J(k)})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}\vee\frac{4}{\tilde{a}}}}\right)+2(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}}\right)

Now using
\gamma_{j}/\gamma_{j+1}\leq 1/(1-\tilde{a}\gamma_{j})
and
2(1-\tilde{a}\gamma_{j})\geq 1
for
j=J(k)\geq j_{0}
, the latter is at most
S_{k}
times

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}\vee\frac{4}{\tilde{a}}+4(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma_{J(k)}}{1-\tilde{a}\gamma_{J(k)}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\tilde{a}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}\vee\frac{4}{\tilde{a}}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\>

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}-q^{0}_{k,\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]}{\gamma_{J(k)}}\vee\frac{4}{\tilde{a}}+4(B+\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j})\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1:k-1}}}\right]-4\gamma_{J(k)}.

This establishes (
100
).

B.5
Proof of Lemma
A.4

Note that, with the notation (
84
), the following inequality is true:

\sup_{t\geq 1}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\Pi}^{0}(q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t})-\alpha}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert}\leq\sup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\Pi}^{0}(q)-\alpha}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert},-\alpha\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j}\leq q\leq B+(1-\alpha)\sup_{j\geq 1}\gamma_{j}}}\right\}\leq\tilde{b}.

(102)

If
q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[0,B]
, we have by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{IER}_{t}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|

\displaystyle=|\Pi_{{\mathcal{C}}_{t},S_{t}}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|

\displaystyle\leq|\Pi_{{\mathcal{C}}_{t},S_{t}}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})|+|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})|

\displaystyle\leq D_{t}+\tilde{b}|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}|,

because
\frac{|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})|}{|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}|}=\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widetilde{\Pi}^{0}(q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t})-\alpha}}\right\rvert}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert}\leq\tilde{b}
since
q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[0,B]
and by (
102
).

B.6
Proof of Theorem
3.2

We establish the following more general result.

Proposition B.4
.

Consider the
OnlineSCI
threshold sequence
(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})_{t\geq 1}
with step sequence
\gamma_{j}=cj^{-\beta}
,
\beta\in(1/2,1)
and starting point
q_{1}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[0,B]
. Consider some function
\Pi^{0}
satisfying Assumptions
7
and
8
with some
q^{0}_{t,\alpha}\in(0,B)
and the corresponding
D_{t}
being defined by (
20
), which is assumed to be bounded by
(\bar{D}_{t})_{t\geq 1}
as in (
23
), with a rate
(\eta_{t})_{t\geq 1}\in(0,1)
.
In addition, we consider
(\underline{J}(t))_{t\geq 1}
and
(\bar{J}(t))_{t\geq 1}
two increasing integer (deterministic) sequences and consider
\omega(t)
given by (
22
).
Then
(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})_{t\geq 1}
is such that there exist
T_{0}=T_{0}(\beta)\geq 1
and
C_{0}=C_{0}(\beta,a,c)>0
such that if
T>t\geq T_{0}
, we have with probability at least
1-3\eta_{t}-\omega(t)-\omega(T+1)
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{T}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq x(t,T,c)+\tilde{a}^{-1}C_{1}\bar{D}_{T}+c(\underline{J}(t))^{-\beta}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}c\sum_{j=\bar{J}(t)}^{\underline{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}}}\right)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x(t,T,c)+B+c}}\right),

(103)

where

\displaystyle x(t,T,c)

\displaystyle:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[c^{2}(\log(T-t)+\log(1/\eta_{T}))\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t)}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}}}\right]^{1/2}.

(104)

Proposition
B.4
implies Theorem
3.2
(case (ii)): indeed, considering the regime of
t(T)
,
\Delta(T)
given in Theorem
3.2
(ii), and apply the two first statements of Lemma
C.6
:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t(T))}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{j=\bar{J}(t(T))}^{\underline{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}\sim\Delta(T)\bar{J}(T)^{-\beta};

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t(T))}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}

\displaystyle\sim\Delta(T)\bar{J}(T)^{-2\beta}.

This means that there exists
T_{0}=T_{0}(\beta)\geq 1
such that for all
T\geq T_{0}
, we have
x(t,T,c)\leq 2c(\log(T-t(T))+\log(1/\eta_{T}))^{1/2}\Delta(T)^{1/2}\bar{J}(T)^{-\beta}
and thus the following holds

\mathbb{P}(|\mathrm{IER}_{T}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|{\mathbf{1}\{q_{T}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[0,B]\}}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{T}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}\rho(T))\geq 1-3\eta_{T}-\omega(t(T))-\omega(T+1),

where we have used Lemma
A.4
. Since
\rho(T)
tends to zero when
T
tends to infinity, we have that
q_{T}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[0,B]
provided that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{T}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}\rho(T)
when
T
is large enough. This leads to (
24
).
Case (i) is completely similar by applying the two last statements of Lemma
C.6
(by taking
t(T)=cT/2
and
\Delta(T)\sim cT/2
).

Let us now prove Proposition
B.4
from the general bound (
78
).
By assumption, for all
T>t\geq 2
, there is an event
\Omega
with probability at least
1-\omega(t)-\omega(T+1)
, for which

\underline{J}(T+1)\leq J(T+1)\leq\bar{J}(T+1)\mbox{ and }\underline{J}(t)\leq J(t)\leq\bar{J}(t),

and thus on
\Omega

\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t)}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}\geq\sum_{j=J(t)}^{J(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}\geq\sum_{j=\bar{J}(t)}^{\underline{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-\beta}\mbox{ , }\sum_{j=J(t+1)}^{J(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}\leq\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t+1)}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}\mbox{ , }J(t)^{-\beta}\leq\underline{J}(t)^{-\beta}.

In addition, we have by (
23
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{t\leq k\leq T}D_{k}\geq C_{1}\bar{D}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{T}\mathbb{P}(D_{k}\geq C_{1}\bar{D}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{T}\mathbb{P}(D_{k}\geq C_{1}\bar{D}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{t}\frac{6}{\pi^{2}}\sum_{k\geq 1}k^{-2}=\eta_{t}.

(105)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Omega\cap\{\max_{t\leq k\leq T-1}|\epsilon_{k-1,T-1}|>x\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{T-1}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Omega\cap\{|\epsilon_{k-1,T-1}|>x\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=t}^{T-1}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\Omega\cap\Big\{c^{2}\sum_{j=J(k)}^{J(T)-1}j^{-2\beta}>y\Big\}\bigg)+2\sum_{k=t}^{T-1}e^{-x^{2}/y}

\displaystyle\leq 2(T-t)\mathbb{P}\bigg(c^{2}\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t)}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}>y\bigg)+2(T-t)e^{-x^{2}/y}\leq 2\eta_{T},

by choosing
y=c^{2}\sum_{j=\underline{J}(t)}^{\bar{J}(T+1)-1}j^{-2\beta}
and
x=x(t,T,c)
given by (
104
).
Combining this with (
78
) and (
105
), we obtain the desired result.

B.7
Proof of Theorem
3.3

We easily check that the considered step sequence
(\gamma_{j})_{j\geq 1}
satisfies the assumption of Theorem
A.3
. The latter hence entails that for any
t_{0}\leq t
with
t_{0}\geq\underline{J}(t_{0})\geq j_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right)^{2}\mid J(t)}}\right]{\mathbf{1}\{\underline{J}(t)\leq J(t)\leq\bar{J}(t)\}}}}\right]+(B+\gamma_{1})^{2}\omega(t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((B+\gamma_{1})^{2}\vee\frac{4}{\tilde{a}}}}\right)\frac{\gamma_{\underline{J}(t)}}{\gamma_{t_{0}}}+4((B+\gamma_{1})\vee 1)\gamma_{\underline{J}(t)}\sum_{k=t_{0}}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S_{k}}}\right]\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}}}\right]+(B+\gamma_{1})^{2}\omega(t),

where we used that by the independence assumption

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S_{k}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1}\dots S_{k-1}}}\right]}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S_{k}}}\right]\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}\mid S_{1}\dots S_{k-1}}}\right]}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S_{k}}}\right]\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}}}\right].

We apply this for
t_{0}=j_{0}
and use Lemma
C.6
to obtain the desired bound for
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]
.
Next, we use Lemma
A.4
with Jensen’s inequality to derive that

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\mathrm{IER}_{t}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|{\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}\in[0,B]\}}}}\right]}}\right)^{2}\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}[D_{t}]+\tilde{b}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]^{1/2}}}\right)^{2}\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}[D_{t}]}}\right)^{2}+2\tilde{b}^{2}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right)^{2}}}\right],

which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathrm{IER}_{t}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha}}\right\rvert{\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}\in[0,B]\}}}}\right]^{2}\leq 2(\mathbb{E}[D_{t}])^{2}+C_{0}\omega(t)+C_{0}(\underline{J}(t))^{-\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[D_{k}}}\right]}}\right).

We finish the proof by using
\mathbb{E}[D_{t}]\leq\widetilde{D}_{t}
and that
(\underline{J}(t))^{-\beta}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}\widetilde{D}_{k}}}\right)\gtrsim\widetilde{D}_{t}\gg(\widetilde{D}_{t})^{2}
.

B.8
Proof of Corollary
4.1

We apply Theorem
3.2
in each time-constant
X
-oriented case:

•

r=1
, that is,
S_{k}(x,q)\equiv 1
:
in that case,
J(t)=t
and thus
\omega(t)=0
with
\underline{J}(t)=t=\bar{J}(t)
and
\eta_{t}=1/t
.
We can apply Theorem
3.2
both in case (i) if
\beta\geq 3/4
and case (ii) with
t(T)=T-\Delta(T)
and
\Delta(T)=1\vee\lfloor T^{4\beta/3}\rfloor
if
\beta<3/4
. This leads to the desired conclusion with
\rho(T)
as in (
3.1
).

•

r<1
and
S(x)=S_{k}(x,q)
does not depend on
q
and
k
: then

J(t+1)-1=\sum_{i=1}^{t}S(X_{i})
follows a binomial distribution of parameter
t,r:=\mathbb{P}(S(X)=1)
.
Thus Bernstein’s inequality gives that, if
\log(t)/t\leq 2r(1-r)
,

\mathbb{P}\Big(|\mathcal{B}(t,r)-tr|\leq 2\sqrt{r(1-r)t\log(t)}\Big)\geq 1-2/t.

As a result, we may choose
\bar{J}(t)\geq 1+\lceil(t-1)r+\{(t-1)\log(t-1)\}^{1/2}\rceil
and
\underline{J}(t)\leq 1+\lfloor(t-1)r-\{(t-1)\log(t-1)\}^{1/2}\rfloor
to obtain the required assumption with
\omega(t)=2/t
.
Hence,
\underline{J}(t)
and
\bar{J}(t)
can be chosen as
\lfloor rt\pm C(\log t)t^{\lambda}\rfloor
for
\lambda=1/2
and some constant
C>0
. Hence Remark
3.1
applies with
\lambda=1/2
, which gives the result.

B.9
Proof of Corollary
4.2

We apply Theorem
3.3
because
S_{k}(X_{k},q_{k})=S(X_{k})
is always independent of
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
for a time-constant selection and in the iid model. This gives the result by taking
\bar{J}(t):=1+\lceil(t-1)r+\{(t-1)\log(t-1)\}^{1/2}\rceil
,
\underline{J}(t):=1+\lfloor(t-1)r-\{(t-1)\log(t-1)\}^{1/2}\rfloor
and
\omega(t)=2/t
, as justified in §
B.8
(which is also a valid choice if
r=1
).

B.10
Proof of Proposition
4.3

We establish the stronger result

\sup_{q\in[0,B]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})>q\>|\>X_{t}=x,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-\Pi^{0}(q)}}\right\rvert\leq M_{1}(1/{\sigma}(x))|\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)|+M_{2}|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)/\sigma(x)-1|,

(106)

which directly leads to (
33
) by an integration.
To establish (
106
), we use that the model is iid, to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})>q\>|\>X_{t}=x,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|\mu(x)-\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)+\sigma(x)\xi|>\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)\bar{\Phi}^{-1}(1/2-q/2)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bar{F}\Bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)}{\sigma(x)}\bar{\Phi}^{-1}(1/2-q/2)+\frac{|\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)|}{\sigma(x)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\>\>\>+\bar{F}\Bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)}{\sigma(x)}\bar{\Phi}^{-1}(1/2-q/2)-\frac{|\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)|}{\sigma(x)}\Bigg),

because the noise as a symmetric distribution.
We now use Lemma
C.8
to deduce

\displaystyle\bar{F}\Bigg(\frac{\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)}{\sigma(x)}\bar{\Phi}^{-1}(1/2-q/2)+\frac{|\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)|}{\sigma(x)}\Bigg)-\bar{F}\Bigg(\bar{\Phi}^{-1}(1/2-q/2)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}{\sigma}^{-1}(x)|\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)|+M_{2}|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)/\sigma(x)-1|,

and thus (
106
).

B.11
Proof of Corollary
4.5

Let us fix
x\in\mathcal{X}
and proves point (i). By (
106
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(x)\mid X_{t}=x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{t}(x,Y_{t})>q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\mid X_{t}=x,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})}}\right]+M_{1}(1/{\sigma}(x))\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)-{\mu}(x)}}\right)^{2}}}\right]^{1/2}+M_{2}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)/\sigma(x)-1}}\right)^{2}}}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha+\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})}}\right\rvert}}\right]+C_{0}(\log t)^{\tau_{0}}t^{-\tau},

by using (
40
), and for some constant
C_{0}>0
(for short, we shall still denote the constant by
C_{0}
in the lines below although this constant should in principle be updated). Now, we use that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})}}\right\rvert}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})}}\right\rvert{\mathbf{1}\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right\rvert\leq\eta_{t}\}}}}\right]+1/t,

by using Corollary
4.4
and letting
\eta_{t}:=C_{0}[\rho(t)+(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}]
. By the mean value theorem, we have that, for
t
large enough, when
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right\rvert\leq\eta_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Pi^{0}(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\Pi^{0}(q_{\alpha}^{0})}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{t}\max_{q\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}-\eta_{t},q_{\alpha}^{0}+\eta_{t}]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|(\Pi^{0})^{\prime}(q)}}\right\rvert=\eta_{t}\max_{q\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}/2,(B+q_{\alpha}^{0})/2]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{f}{\phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1-q}{2}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}\eta_{t}.

This gives (
41
) of point (i).

Now, let us prove point (ii). We relate
|{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(x)|=2\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big)
to
|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|=2\sigma(x)\overline{F}^{-1}(\alpha/2)
by using Corollary
4.4
: first note that applying the in-probability bound (
37
), we have for
t
large enough, for all
M>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(x)|\wedge M}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big){\mathbf{1}\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right\rvert\leq\eta_{t}\}}}}\right]+M/t

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big){\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}/2,(1+q_{\alpha}^{0})/2]\}}}}\right]+M/t.

Now, by the triangle inequality and the mean-value theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big){\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}/2,(1+q_{\alpha}^{0})/2]\}}}}\right]-\sigma(x)\overline{F}^{-1}(\alpha/2)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)-\sigma(x))\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big){\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}/2,(1+q_{\alpha}^{0})/2]\}}}}\right]

\displaystyle+\sigma(x)\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}+1}{2}\Big)-\Phi^{-1}\Big(\frac{q_{\alpha}^{0}+1}{2}\Big)}}\right){\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}\in[q_{\alpha}^{0}/2,(1+q_{\alpha}^{0})/2]\}}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)-\sigma(x)}}\right\rvert}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right\rvert}}\right]}}\right),

because
\sigma(x)
is uniformly upper-bounded by Assumption
3
.
Combining the latter with the previous display, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(x)|\wedge M}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|+M/t+C_{0}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\sigma}_{t}(x)-\sigma(x)}}\right)^{2}}}\right]^{1/2}+C_{0}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q_{\alpha}^{0}}}\right)^{2}}}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|+M/t+C_{0}(\log t)^{\tau_{0}}t^{-\tau}+C_{0}[(\log t)^{\tau_{0}}t^{1-\tau-\beta}]^{1/2},

by using the in-expectation bound (
38
) of Corollary
4.4
.
This gives (
42
).

Finally, we prove (iii). Consider any procedure
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x),t\geq 1,x\in\mathcal{X}
with the conditional coverage property
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)\mid X_{t}=x}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha-\eta^{\prime}_{t}
. Since
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x),x\in\mathcal{X}
satisfies (
39
), we have
almost surely, for all
y\in\mathcal{Y}
,

(f_{Y|X=x}(y)-c^{*}(x))({\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)\}}-{\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)\}})\geq 0.

Hence, by integrating with respect to the Lebesgue measure,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}(f_{Y|X=x}(y)-c^{*}(x))({\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)\}}-{\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)\}})dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{Y|X=x}(y){\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)\}}dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{Y|X=x}(y){\mathbf{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)\}})dy-c^{*}(x)(|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|-|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)|).

Now, since
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)\mid X_{t}=x}}\right)=1-\alpha
(because the distribution of
Y_{t}
given
X_{t}=x
is continuous) and by assumption
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)\mid X_{t}=x}}\right)\geq 1-\alpha-\eta^{\prime}_{t}
, we integrate wrt
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, to obtain

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{\prime}_{t}-c^{*}(x)(|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)|}}\right]).

Since
c^{*}(x)>0
and
\sigma(x)
is uniformly lower bounded, this gives

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)|\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{t}(x)|}}\right]+C_{0}\eta^{\prime}_{t},

(107)

which means that, up to
C_{0}\eta^{\prime}_{t}
, the prediction set
{\mathcal{C}}^{*}(x)
is pointwise better than
{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(x)
.
Combining the latter with (
42
) concludes the proof.

B.12
Proof of Corollary
4.6

Here we have
\Pi_{{\mathcal{C}}_{t},S_{t}}(q)=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})>q\mid\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\geq y_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}}}\right)
with
V_{t}(x,y)
as in (
6
)
and with the benchmark function
\Pi^{0}
as in (
32
).

Consider for
r_{t}:=\sqrt{C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}
the probability

\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}):=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-\mu(X_{t})}}\right\rvert>r_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right).

(108)

By (
35
) and since by Markov’s inequality
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-\mu(X_{t})}}\right\rvert\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]/r_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})>r_{t}}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-\mu(X_{t})}}\right\rvert\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]>r^{2}_{t}}}\right)\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}.

(109)

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\geq y_{0}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mu(X_{t})\geq y_{0}}}\right)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-\mu(X_{t})}}\right\rvert>r_{t}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mu(X_{t})-y_{0}}}\right\rvert\leq r_{t}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})+2M_{4}r_{t}\leq(1+2M_{4})r_{t},

(110)

for
t
larger than some
T_{0}
(only depending on the neighborhood of Assumption
4
) and on the event where
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
.

Now, we would like to apply Theorem
3.2
, which requires to establish (
23
) and to find sequences bounding
J(t)
as in (
22
).

Bounding
D_{t}

Let us first show for
t\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}(D_{t}>C_{0}r^{2}_{t})\leq\frac{6}{\pi^{2}t^{3}},

(111)

for some constant
C_{0}>0
.
From (
110
), we have for
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated
T_{0}
), that
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})\geq y_{0}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}}}\right)\geq r_{0}:=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mu(X_{t})\geq y_{0}}}\right)/2>0
, provided that
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
. Hence, by applying (
33
) in Proposition
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle D_{t}\leq\|\Pi_{{\mathcal{C}}_{t},S_{t}}-\Pi^{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\>\mathbb{E}[M_{1}|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-{\mu}(X_{t})|+(M_{2}\vee 1)|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(X_{t})-{\sigma}(X_{t})|\>|\>S_{t}(X_{t})=1,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq r_{0}^{-1}M_{3}\>\mathbb{E}[M_{1}|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-{\mu}(X_{t})|+(M_{2}\vee 1)|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(X_{t})-{\sigma}(X_{t})|\>|\>\mathcal{F}_{t-1}],

for
t\geq T_{0}
and provided that
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
.
As a result, we obtain (by adjusting appropriately the constant
C_{0}>0
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(D_{t}>C_{0}r^{2}_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-{\mu}(X_{t})|+|\hat{\sigma}_{t}(X_{t})-{\sigma}(X_{t})|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]\geq C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})>r_{t}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}+\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}},

by using (
35
) and (
109
), which shows (
111
).

Bounding
J(t)

Let us now prove for
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
)

\mathbb{P}\big(J(t)\notin[\underline{J}(t),\bar{J}(t)]\big)\leq 1/t,

(112)

for functions
\underline{J}(t)<\bar{J}(t)
to be suitably chosen.
By applying Lemma
C.4
(
134
), we have that for all
t\geq T_{0}
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|J(t)-(T_{0}-1)-\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\mu}_{k}(X_{k})\geq y_{0}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)}}\right\rvert\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{t\log(4t)}{2}}}\right)^{1/2}}}\right)\leq 1/(2t).

In addition, we have by (
110
) and (
109
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\hat{\mu}_{k}(X_{k})\geq y_{0}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mu}(X_{k})\geq y_{0}}}\right)}}\right\rvert>(1+2M_{4})\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}r_{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exists k\in[T_{0},t]\>:\>\varepsilon_{k}(\mathcal{F}_{k-1})\leq r_{k}}}\right)\leq\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}\frac{3}{\pi^{2}k^{3}}\leq 1/(2t),

by adjusting again the constant
T_{0}
.
Note that

(1+2M_{4})\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}r_{k}=(1+2M_{4})(C_{1})^{1/2}\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}C_{1}(\log k)^{\nu_{0}/2}k^{-\nu/2}\leq C_{0}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{1-\nu/2},

for some constant well chosen constant
C_{0}>0
. By adjusting
C_{0}>0
, this gives (
112
) for

\displaystyle\underline{J}(t)

\displaystyle=t\>\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mu}(X)\geq y_{0}}}\right)-C_{0}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{1-\nu/2},;

\displaystyle\bar{J}(t)

\displaystyle=t\>\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mu}(X)\geq y_{0}}}\right)+C_{0}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{1-\nu/2}.

because
(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{1-\nu/2}\gg\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{t\log(4t)}{2}}}\right)^{1/2}
.

Conclusion

We apply Theorem
3.2
with Remark
3.1
and
\lambda=1-\nu/2
.

B.13
Proof of Corollary
4.7

We check the assumptions of Theorems
3.2
and
3.3
.
Obtaining (
22
) is obvious because there is always selection, so that we set
\bar{J}(t)=\underline{J}(t)=J(t)=t
and
\omega(t)=0
. For
D_{t}
, we use (
106
) in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, see Section
B.10
, which also applies in the auto-regressive model (
46
) because
Y_{t}\mid X_{t}
follows a Gaussian distribution with mean
\mu(X_{t})=\langle\varphi;X_{t}\rangle
and variance
1
in that model.
This leads to

D_{t}\leq\sup_{q\in[0,B]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})>q\>|\>\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-\Pi^{0}(q)}}\right\rvert\leq M_{1}|\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})-{\mu}(X_{t})|,

with
M_{1}=\Phi^{\prime}(0)
and because
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\{|x|\Phi^{\prime}(x)\}<\infty
.
Hence, (
35
) with
\nu_{0}=3/2
,
\nu=1/2
provides (
23
) with
\bar{D}_{t}=O((\log t)^{3/2}t^{-1/2})
. In addition, (
36
) with
\tau_{0}=0
,
\tau=1/2
provides
\mathbb{E}[D_{t}]\leq\widetilde{D}_{t}
with
\widetilde{D}_{t}=O(t^{-1/2})
.

To conclude, for the in-probability result, we apply Theorem
3.2
case (i) with Remark
3.1
. For the in-expectation result, we apply Theorem
3.3
.

B.14
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Let us denote
\Pi_{t}
for
\Pi_{{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t},S^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}_{t}}
and
q_{t}
for
q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}
for short.

We apply Theorem
3.2
(case (i)), or more precisely (
27
) in the refined version presented in Remark
3.3
, on events
\Omega_{t}
that we now specify.
Consider any
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
. Then by definition (
49
), there exists
\bar{q}=\bar{q}(\delta^{\prime})\in[0,B^{*})
and
T_{0}=T_{0}(\delta^{\prime})\geq 1
such that
\mathbb{P}(\sup_{T\geq T_{0}}q_{T}>\bar{q})\leq\delta^{\prime}
, that is,
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}
for the event

\Omega:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\forall t\geq T_{0},q_{t}\leq\bar{q}}}\right\}=\cap_{t\geq T_{0}}\Omega_{t},\>\>\>\mbox{ with }\Omega_{t}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\forall k\in[T_{0},t],q_{k}\leq\bar{q}}}\right\}\in\mathcal{F}_{t-1}.

(113)

In addition, for
r_{t}:=\sqrt{C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}
, we let

\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}):=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max_{y\in[K]}|\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},y)-\bar{V}(X_{t},y)|>r_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right).

(114)

By (
56
) and since by Markov’s inequality,
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\max_{y\in[K]}|\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},y)-\bar{V}(X_{t},y)|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]/r_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})>r_{t}}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\max_{y\in[K]}|\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},y)-\bar{V}(X_{t},y)|\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]>r^{2}_{t}}}\right)\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}.

(115)

Also note that since

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|W_{t}(X_{t})-W(X_{t})}}\right\rvert=\Big|\max_{y\in[K]}V_{t}(X_{t},y)-\max_{y\in[K]}V(X_{t},y)\Big|\leq\max_{y\in[K]}|\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},y)-\bar{V}(X_{t},y)|,

(116)

bound (
114
) also provides
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{t}(X_{t})-W(X_{t})|>r_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)\leq\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})
almost surely.

Bounding
D_{t}

Let us show that for all
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
),

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{t}>C_{0}r_{t},\Omega_{t}}}\right)\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}.

(117)

For this, we have for all
q\in[0,\bar{q}]
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{t}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})\mid W_{t}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},Y_{t})\mid X_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)\mid W_{t}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\bar{V}(X_{t},\arg\max\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},\cdot))\mid W_{t}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right],

where
\arg\max\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},\cdot)
denotes
\arg\max_{y\in[K]}\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},y)
.
Since
\bar{V}(X_{t},\arg\max\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},\cdot))\leq\max_{y\in[K]}V(X_{t},y)=W(X_{t})
by definition, we have on the event where
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
,

\displaystyle 1-\Pi_{t}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\bar{V}(X_{t},\arg\max\bar{V}_{t}(X_{t},\cdot))\mid W_{t}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\mid W_{t}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{W_{t}(X_{t})>q\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>q\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q+r_{t}}}\right)-r_{t}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]+r_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}}}\right)-C_{0}r_{t}}}}\right]+r_{t},

because since the density of
W(X_{t})
is bounded we have
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q-r_{t}<W(X_{t})\leq q+r_{t}}}\right)\leq C_{0}r_{t}
,
and by assuming that
C_{0},T_{0}
are large enough so that
r_{t}\leq(1-\bar{q})/2
and
C_{0}r_{t}<\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>\bar{q}}}\right)/2
(by Assumption
5
).
Since
1/(1-u)\leq 1+2u
for all
u\in(0,1/2]
, it entails that on the event where
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
,

\displaystyle 1-\Pi_{t}(q)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+2\frac{C_{0}r_{t}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}}}\right)}}}\right)}}\right]+r_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+2\frac{C_{0}r_{t}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>\bar{q}}}\right)}}}\right)\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q-r_{t}}}\right)}}}\right]+\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+2\frac{C_{0}r_{t}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>\bar{q}}}\right)}}}\right)(1-\Pi^{0}(q-r_{t}))+r_{t}.

Since
\sup_{q\in[0,\bar{q}]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Pi^{0}(q-r_{t})-\Pi^{0}(q+r_{t})}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}r_{t}
by Lemma
C.7
, we have finally (still by adjusting the constant
C_{0}
),

\sup_{q\in[0,\bar{q}]}(\Pi^{0}(q)-\Pi_{t}(q)){\mathbf{1}\{\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}\}}\leq C_{0}r_{t}

Similarly, we obtain
\sup_{q\in[0,\bar{q}]}(\Pi_{t}(q)-\Pi^{0}(q)){\mathbf{1}\{\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}\}}\leq C_{0}r_{t}

which gives finally, for
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}

|\Pi_{t}(q_{t})-\Pi^{0}(q_{t})|{\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}\in[0,B]\}}{\mathbf{1}\{\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}\}}\leq C_{0}r_{t},

(118)

which gives (
117
) with (
115
).

Bounding
J(t)

Let us now prove for
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
)

\mathbb{P}\big(J(t)>\underline{J}(t),\Omega_{t}\big)\leq 1/t,

(119)

for a function
\underline{J}(t)
to be suitably chosen.
By applying Lemma
C.4
(
134
), we have that for all
t\geq T_{0}
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(J(t)\geq T_{0}-1+\sum_{k=T_{0}}^{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)+x}}\right)\leq e^{-2x^{2}/t}.

In addition, by successively applying Lemma
C.5
(
135
), and (
116
)-(
114
), we have almost surely

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}r_{t}+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{t}(X_{t})-W(X_{t})|>r_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}r_{t}+\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}).

This means that, for all
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-C_{0}r_{t}-\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})

(120)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X)>\bar{q}}}\right)-C_{0}r_{t}-\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X)>\bar{q}}}\right)-(C_{0}+1)r_{t},

for which we assumed in addition that
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
in the last inequality.
By (
115
), this means that (
119
) holds for
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
), by letting

\displaystyle\underline{J}(t)

\displaystyle:=t\>\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X)>\bar{q}}}\right)/2,

(121)

which is of order
t
by Assumption
5
.

Conclusion

We apply Theorem
3.2
(case (i), Remark
3.3
), to obtain (
58
).

B.15
Proof for §
5.2

B.15.1
Optimality of the oracle procedure

Proposition B.5
.

Consider the iid classification model (
53
) satisfying Assumption
5
with
W(x):=\max_{y\in[K]}\mathbb{P}(Y=y\mid X=x)
and support values
1/K\leq\kappa\leq B^{*}\leq 1
, and an error level
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that
1-B^{*}<\alpha<1-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X)}}\right]
. Then any selective classification procedure
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))_{t\geq 1}
(for which each function
S_{t}(\cdot)
and
\hat{Y}_{t}(\cdot)
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable) is dominated by the oracle procedure
(S^{*}(X_{t}),Y^{*}(X_{t}))_{t\geq 1}
given by (
59
)-(
60
) in the following sense: if for all
t\geq T_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+z
, for
z\geq 0
, then for all
t\geq T_{0}

\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S_{t}(X_{t})\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=Y^{*}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\big]+zq^{0}_{\alpha}/(1-\alpha-q^{0}_{\alpha}).

(122)

For
z=0
, this result is (essentially) proved in Theorem 1 of
Zhao and Su, 2023
. Hence, Proposition
B.5
is an online extension, accommodating the case where
z>0
.

Proof.

First note that by (
51
), we have
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq{Y}^{*}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1)=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[1-W(X_{t})\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1}}\right]=1-\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t})\mid W(X_{t})>q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right]=\alpha
and that
q^{0}_{\alpha}<1-\alpha
.
Also, by denoting

f:x\in(\alpha,1)\mapsto f(x)=(x-\alpha)/(1-x),

(123)

which is continuous, positive and increasing, we have

S^{*}(X_{t})={\mathbf{1}\{1-W(X_{t})<1-q^{0}_{\alpha}\}}={\mathbf{1}\{f(1-W(X_{t}))<c^{*}\}}={\mathbf{1}\{1-W(X_{t})-\alpha-c^{*}W(X_{t})<0\}},

for
c^{*}:=f(1-q^{0}_{\alpha})>0
.
Now consider any selective classification procedure
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))_{t\geq 1}
, for which each function
S_{t}(\cdot)
and
\hat{Y}_{t}(\cdot)
are
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable.
Hence, almost surely, we have

(1-W(X_{t})-\alpha-c^{*}W(X_{t}))(S^{*}(X_{t})-S_{t}(X_{t}))\leq 0.

Rearranging this inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle c^{*}(W(X_{t})S^{*}(X_{t})-W(X_{t})S_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq(1-W(X_{t})-\alpha)S^{*}(X_{t})-(1-W(X_{t})-\alpha)S_{t}(X_{t}),

and thus because
W(X_{t})\geq\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))
,

\displaystyle c^{*}(W(X_{t})S^{*}(X_{t})-\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))S_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq(1-W(X_{t})-\alpha)S^{*}(X_{t})-(1-\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))-\alpha)S_{t}(X_{t}).

By integrating the latter in
X_{t}
and
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, and by assuming
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+z
, we obtain

\displaystyle c^{*}(\mathbb{E}(W(X_{t})S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}(\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))S_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}((1-W(X_{t})-\alpha)S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}((1-\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))-\alpha)S_{t}(X_{t})),

that is,

\displaystyle c^{*}(\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}={Y}^{*}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S_{t}(X_{t})))\geq 0-z\mathbb{P}(S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\geq-z,

because

\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\mid X_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t-1})=1-\bar{V}(X_{t},\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))

and

\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}={Y}^{*}_{t}(X_{t})\mid X_{t})=W(X_{t}).

This gives (
122
).
∎

B.15.2
Proof of Corollary
5.2

To prove Corollary
5.2
, we use Theorem
5.1
.
Consider any
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
,
\bar{q}=\bar{q}(\delta^{\prime})\in[0,B^{*})
and
T_{0}=T_{0}(\delta^{\prime})\geq 1
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}
for the event
\Omega=\cap_{t\geq T_{0}}\Omega_{t}
in (
113
).

From Theorem
5.1
(
58
) (and its proof) recall that we have for the rate
\rho(t)=(\log t)^{1/2}t^{1/2-\beta}+t^{1-\beta}
,

\mathbb{P}(|\mathrm{IER}_{t}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}(\rho(t)+(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{-\nu/2})\mid\Omega_{t})\geq 1-C_{0}/t.

(124)

Let
\eta_{t}=\rho(t)+(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{-\nu/2}
for short and let us now prove (i). We have for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}/t,

by using (
124
), which gives (i) because
1/t\ll\eta_{t}
(by updating the constant
C_{0}>0
).

Let us now prove (ii). We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t}}}\right)\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})(1-\alpha-C_{0}\eta_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}={Y}^{*}(X_{t})\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t}}}\right)-C_{0}\eta_{t},

by (
51
) and because
\Omega_{t}
is independent of
(X_{t},Y_{t})
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}={Y}^{*}(X_{t})\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\>\>\>-C_{0}\eta_{t}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})}}\right).

Now let us bound the last term by using some of the steps of the proof in §
B.14
. By (
120
), and with the notation therein, we have
for all
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}
and
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-(C_{0}+1)r_{t},

with
r_{t}=\sqrt{C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}
. This entails

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0},\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(W_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t},\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{\Omega_{t}\}}(\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}))}}\right]

\displaystyle\>\>\>+\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{\Omega_{t}\}}(\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(W_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}))}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0})-\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}+C_{0}\eta_{t})+C_{0}/t+(C_{0}+1)r_{t}+\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}\Big)\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{t}),

by (
115
) and (
124
). Since the density of
W(X_{t})
is continuous on
[\kappa,B^{*}]
, it is upper bounded, and we have
\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0})-\mathbb{P}(W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}+C_{0}\eta_{t})\leq C_{0}\eta_{t}
.
This gives
\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})\leq C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t}
and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}={Y}^{*}(X_{t})\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t},

which provides (using
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{t}^{c})\leq\delta^{\prime}
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}={Y}^{*}(X_{t})\}}S^{*}(X_{t})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\big]+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t}+\delta^{\prime},

This gives (ii).

Finally, we prove (iii).
By Proposition
B.5
, for any
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t}))_{t\geq 1}
such that for all
t\geq T_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\neq\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+\delta^{\prime}+\eta_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S_{t}(X_{t})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[W(X_{t}){\mathbf{1}\{W(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}\}}}}\right]+q^{0}_{\alpha}(\delta^{\prime}+\eta_{t})/(1-\alpha-q^{0}_{\alpha}).

This finally gives

\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S_{t}(X_{t})\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=\hat{Y}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\big]+C_{0}\rho(t)+C_{0}r_{t}+C(\delta^{\prime}+\eta_{t}),

where
C_{0}
depends on
\delta^{\prime}
and
C
only depends on
\alpha
.
This is (
61
) because
r_{t}=O(\eta_{t})
.

B.16
Proof of Theorem
5.3

The proof is analogue to the proof of Theorem
5.1
. We consider the events in (
113
) and for
r_{t}:=\sqrt{C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}
, we let

\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}):=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})}}\right\rvert>r_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right).

(125)

By (
66
) and Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})>r_{t}}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})}}\right\rvert\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]>r^{2}_{t}}}\right)\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}.

(126)

Bounding
D_{t}

Let us show that for all
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
),

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{t}>C_{0}r_{t},\Omega_{t}}}\right)\leq\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}.

(127)

For this, we have for all
q\in[0,\bar{q}]
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{t}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}=0\mid 1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})\mid 1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]

By definition, we have

\displaystyle 1-\Pi_{t}(q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})\mid 1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})>q,\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right].

Hence, we can proceed exactly as in the proof of Theorem
5.1
with
W(X_{t}):=1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})
and
W_{t}(X_{t}):=1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}_{t}}(X_{t})
to obtain for
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}

|\Pi_{t}(q_{t})-\Pi^{0}(q_{t})|{\mathbf{1}\{q_{t}\in[0,B]\}}{\mathbf{1}\{\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}\}}\leq C_{0}r_{t},

(128)

which gives (
127
) with (
126
).

Bounding
J(t)

Again, we proceed exactly as in the proof of Theorem
5.1
to prove that, for
t\geq T_{0}
(with an updated constant
T_{0}
)

\mathbb{P}\big(J(t)>\underline{J}(t),\Omega_{t}\big)\leq 1/t,

(129)

for

\displaystyle\underline{J}(t)

\displaystyle:=t\>\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>\bar{q}}}\right)/2.

(130)

Conclusion

We apply Theorem
3.2
(case (i), Remark
3.3
), to obtain (
67
).

B.17
Proof for §
5.3

B.17.1
Optimality of the oracle procedure

Proposition B.6
.

Consider the iid online testing model (
62
) satisfying Assumption
5
with the test statistics
W(x):=1-{\mathrm{lfdr}}(x)
(
64
) and support values
0\leq\kappa<B^{*}\leq 1
, and an error level
\alpha\in(0,1)
, such that
1-B^{*}<\alpha<\mathbb{P}(Y=0)
. Then any online testing procedure
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\{1\})_{t\geq 1}
(for which
S_{t}(\cdot)
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable) is dominated by the oracle procedure
(S^{*}(X_{t}),\{1\})_{t\geq 1}
given by (
68
) in the following sense: if for all
t\geq T_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+z
, for
z\geq 0
, then for all
t\geq T_{0}

\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S_{t}(X_{t})\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\big]+zq^{0}_{\alpha}/(1-\alpha-q^{0}_{\alpha}).

(131)

For
z=0
, this result is (essentially) proved in
Sun and Cai, 2007
. Hence, Proposition
B.6
is an online extension, accommodating the case where
z>0
.

Proof.

The proof is analogue to the proof of Proposition
B.5
, and we provide it here for completeness.
First note that by (
51
), we have
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1)=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1}}\right]=\alpha
and
q^{0}_{\alpha}<1-\alpha
.
Also, by considering
f
in (
123
), we have

S^{*}(X_{t})={\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})<1-q^{0}_{\alpha}\}}={\mathbf{1}\{f(\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t}))<c^{*}\}}={\mathbf{1}\{\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha-c^{*}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})))<0\}},

for
c^{*}:=f(1-q^{0}_{\alpha})>0
.
Now consider any online testing procedure
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\{1\})_{t\geq 1}
, for which
S_{t}(\cdot)
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable.
Hence, almost surely, we have

(\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha-c^{*}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})))(S^{*}(X_{t})-S_{t}(X_{t}))\leq 0.

Rearranging this inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle c^{*}((1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})))S^{*}(X_{t})-(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t}))S_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq(\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha)S^{*}(X_{t})-(\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha)S_{t}(X_{t}).

By integrating the latter in
X_{t}
and
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
, and by assuming
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+z
, we obtain

\displaystyle c^{*}(\mathbb{E}((1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t}))S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}((1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t}))S_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}((\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha)S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}((\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})-\alpha)S_{t}(X_{t})),

that is,

\displaystyle c^{*}(\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t}))-\mathbb{E}({\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S_{t}(X_{t})))\geq 0-z\mathbb{P}(S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\geq-z,

because

\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid X_{t},\mathcal{F}_{t-1})=\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})
.
This gives (
131
).
∎

B.17.2
Proof of Corollary
5.4

The proof is analogue to the proof of Corollary
5.2
, and we report it here for completeness. For this, let us use Theorem
5.3
.
Consider any
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
,
\bar{q}=\bar{q}(\delta^{\prime})\in[0,B)
and
T_{0}=T_{0}(\delta^{\prime})\geq 1
such that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega)\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}
for the event
\Omega=\cap_{t\geq T_{0}}\Omega_{t}
in (
113
).

From Theorem
5.3
(
67
) (and its proof) recall that we have for the rate
\rho(t)=(\log t)^{1/2}t^{1/2-\beta}+t^{1-\beta}
,

\mathbb{P}(|\mathrm{IER}_{t}(\mathcal{R}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})-\alpha|+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}-q^{0}_{\alpha}}}\right\rvert\leq C_{0}(\rho(t)+(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{-\nu/2})\mid\Omega_{t})\geq 1-C_{0}/t,

(132)

Let
\eta_{t}:=\rho(t)+(\log t)^{\nu_{0}/2}t^{-\nu/2}
and let us now prove (i). We have for
t\geq T_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}/t,

by using (
132
), which gives (i) because
1/t\ll\eta_{t}
(by updating the constant
C_{0}>0
).

Let us now prove (ii). We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}=1\mid S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t}}}\right)\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})(1-\alpha-C_{0}\eta_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y_{t}=1\mid S^{*}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t}}}\right)-C_{0}\eta_{t},

by (i) and (
51
), and because
\Omega_{t}
is independent of
(X_{t},Y_{t})
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\>\>\>-C_{0}\eta_{t}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})}}\right).

Now let us bound the last term by using some of the steps of the proof in §
B.16
(and the notation therein).
For all
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}}_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-C_{0}r_{t}-\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}).

Hence, we have
for all
t\geq T_{0}
, on the event
\Omega_{t}
and
\varepsilon_{t}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1})\leq r_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}}_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right)-(C_{0}+1)r_{t},

with
r_{t}=\sqrt{C_{1}(\log t)^{\nu_{0}}t^{-\nu}}
. This entails

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1,\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0},\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}}_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t},\Omega_{t})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{\Omega_{t}\}}(\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}))}}\right]

\displaystyle\>\>\>+\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{\Omega_{t}\}}(\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})-\mathbb{P}(1-\widehat{\mathrm{lfdr}}_{t}(X_{t})>q_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}))}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0})-\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}+C_{0}\eta_{t})+C_{0}/t+(C_{0}+1)r_{t}+\frac{3}{\pi^{2}t^{3}}\Big)\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{t}),

by (
126
) and (
132
).
Since the density of the random variale
\mathrm{lfdr}(X)
is continuous on
[0,1]
, it is upper bounded, and we have
\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0})-\mathbb{P}(1-\mathrm{lfdr}(X_{t})>q_{\alpha}^{0}+C_{0}\eta_{t})\leq C_{0}\eta_{t}
.
This gives
\mathbb{P}(S^{*}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})-\mathbb{P}(S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})=1\mid\Omega_{t})\leq C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t}
and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid\Omega_{t}\big]+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t},

which provides (using
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{t}^{c})\leq\delta^{\prime}
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{t}(X_{t})\big]+C_{0}\eta_{t}+C_{0}r_{t}+\delta^{\prime},

This gives (ii) because
r_{t}=O(\eta_{t})
.

Finally, we prove (iii).
By Proposition
B.6
, for any
(S_{t}(X_{t}),\{1\})_{t\geq 1}
such that for all
t\geq T_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=0\mid S_{t}(X_{t})=1)\leq\alpha+\delta^{\prime}+\eta_{t}
(for a possibly different rate
\eta_{t}
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S_{t}(X_{t})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[{\mathbf{1}\{Y_{t}=1\}}S^{*}(X_{t})\big]+q^{0}_{\alpha}(\delta^{\prime}+\eta_{t})/(1-\alpha-q^{0}_{\alpha}),

This finally gives (
69
) by (ii).

Appendix C
Auxiliary results

C.1
Specific lemmas

Proposition C.1
.

Consider any procedure
{\mathcal{R}}=((S_{t})_{t\geq 1},({\mathcal{C}}_{t})_{t\geq 1})
producing non-trivial prediction sets on the selection, that is, such that
{\mathcal{C}}_{t}\neq\mathcal{Y}
whenever
S_{t}=1
. Then it has the following intrinsic limitations (with the coverage error (
9
)):

(i)

If an FCP bound of the type (
16
) is true, then the procedure should stop selection after some time.
More formally, assume that for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and any sequence
(X_{t},Y_{t})_{t\geq 1}
, we have

\mathrm{FCP}_{t}(\mathcal{R})\leq\alpha+\Psi(J(t))
for all
t\geq 1
, with
J(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}S_{i}+1
and
\lim_{j\to\infty}\Psi(j)=0
. Then there exists a sequence
(X_{t},Y_{t})_{t\geq 1}
such that
\sup_{t\geq 1}J(t)<+\infty
;

(ii)

No FCP bound of the type (
16
) that always converges to some
\alpha\in(0,1)
can hold. More formally, assume that for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and any sequence
(X_{t},Y_{t})_{t\geq 1}
,
we have
\mathrm{FCP}_{t}(\mathcal{R})\leq\alpha+\varepsilon(t)
for all
t\geq 1
, for some sequence
(\varepsilon(t))_{t\geq 1}
. Then there exists a sequence
(X_{t},Y_{t})_{t\geq 1}
such that
(\varepsilon(t))_{t\geq 1}
does not converges to
0
.

Proof.

By assumption, since the procedure is non-trivial at each time, we can recursively build a sequence
(X_{t},Y_{t})_{t\geq 1}
such that
Y_{t}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t},(X_{i},Y_{i})_{1\leq i\leq t-1})
for all
t\geq 1
and thus
\mathrm{FCP}_{t}({\mathcal{R}})=1
for all
t\geq 1
. Hence, (i) and (ii) follow.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Consider the
OnlineSCI
threshold sequence
(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})_{t\geq 1}
and let
t_{0}\geq 1
be the smallest (deterministic) integer for which
q_{0}+(1-\alpha)(\gamma_{1}+\dots+\gamma_{t_{0}})\geq B
. Then we have
q_{T}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}=q_{t_{0}}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}
for all
T\geq t_{0}
on the event where one always wrongly selects at time
\leq t_{0}-1
, that is,

\cap_{1\leq t\leq t_{0}-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{W_{t}(X_{t})>q^{\prime}_{t},Y_{t}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t},q^{\prime}_{t})}}\right\},

(133)

for
q^{\prime}_{t}:=q_{0}+(1-\alpha)(\gamma_{1}+\dots+\gamma_{t})
,
1\leq t\leq t_{0}-1
.
Moreover, the probability of (
133
) is positive in the iid model, if
W_{t}(\cdot)
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(\cdot,\cdot)
are deterministic functions,
\mathbb{P}(W_{t}(X)>q)>0
for all
q<B
and
1\leq t\leq t_{0}-1
and
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t},q)\mid X_{t})
is positive almost surely.

Proof.

The first statement is obvious from the definition of
OnlineSCI
. The second one is true because by the iid assumption, and since each
q^{\prime}_{t}
is deterministic

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\cap_{1\leq t\leq t_{0}-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{W_{t}(X_{t})>q^{\prime}_{t},Y_{t}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t},q^{\prime}_{t})}}\right\}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{t}(X)>q^{\prime}_{t},Y\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X,q^{\prime}_{t})}}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{t=1}^{t_{0}-1}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X,q^{\prime}_{t})\mid X}}\right){\mathbf{1}\{W_{t}(X)>q^{\prime}_{t}\}}}}\right].

Now, if there exists
t\in[1,t_{0}-1]
with
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X,q^{\prime}_{t})\mid X}}\right){\mathbf{1}\{W_{t}(X)>q^{\prime}_{t}\}}}}\right]=0
, we have almost surely that either
W_{t}(X)>q^{\prime}_{t}
or
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Y\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X,q^{\prime}_{t})\mid X}}\right)=0
. Since
q^{\prime}_{t}<B
for
t\leq t_{0}-1
, this is impossible with the assumption of the lemma. This shows that each term is positive and thus the probability of (
133
) is also positive.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Incompressibility of
\delta
)
.

Consider the
OnlineSCI
threshold sequence
(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})_{t\geq 1}
,
\delta
given by (
49
) and any
q^{0}\in(0,B^{*})
with
B^{*}
as in Assumption
5
. Then
\mathbb{P}(\lim_{t}q_{t}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}=q^{0})\leq 1-\delta
.

Proof.

Assume
\delta>0
(otherwise the result is clearly true) and take
0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta
.
By definition, we have for all
q\in[0,B^{*})
and
t\geq 1
,
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{T\geq t}q_{T}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}>q}}\right)\geq\delta^{\prime}
. Hence taking any
q\in(q^{0},B^{*})
(which is possible because
q^{0}\in(0,B^{*})
), we have

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{t}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}\nrightarrow q^{0}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\lim_{t}\sup_{T\geq t}q_{T}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}\geq q}}\right)=\lim_{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{T\geq t}q_{T}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}\geq q}}\right)\geq\delta^{\prime}.

Finally, we obtained that for all
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,\delta)
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\lim_{t}q_{t}^{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}=q^{0})\leq 1-\delta^{\prime}
, which shows the result.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Concentration of
J(t)
)
.

Consider the threshold
(q_{t}^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}})_{t\geq 1}
of
OnlineSCI
, used with any selection rule
(S_{t}(\cdot))_{t\geq 1}
. Then, without any model assumption, we have for all
t\geq 1
,
x>0
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Big|J(t)-\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)\Big|\geq x}}\right)\leq 2e^{-2x^{2}/t},

(134)

where
J(t)
is defined by (
13
).

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle J(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}{\mathbf{1}\{S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathbf{1}\{S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\}}-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)}}\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle=M_{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k},q^{{\mbox{{\tiny SCI}}}}_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right).

The process
(M_{t})_{t\geq 1}
is a square integrable martingale adapted to the filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 1}
with
\langle M\rangle_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Delta M_{t}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}}}\right]\leq t/4
,
-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)=:A_{k}\leq\Delta M_{t}\leq B_{k}:=1-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(S_{k}(X_{k})=1\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)
and
\mathcal{D}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}(B_{k}-A_{k})^{2}\leq t
. Hence, Lemma
C.10
provides (using that
2\langle M\rangle_{n}+\mathcal{D}_{n}\leq y=3t/2
almost surely) that for all
t\geq 1
,
x>0
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t}\geq x}}\right)\leq e^{-2x^{2}/t}.

This leads to (
134
).
∎

Lemma C.5
.

Consider any sequence threshold
(q_{t})_{t\geq 1}
such that each
q_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
measurable, and a selection rule of the form
S_{t}(x,q)={\mathbf{1}\{W_{t}(x)>q\}}
for some
\mathcal{F}_{t-1}
-measurable function
W_{t}:x\in\mathcal{X}\mapsto W_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}
. In the iid model, consider any (deterministic) function
W:x\in\mathcal{X}\mapsto W(x)\in\mathbb{R}
and assume that the random variable
W(X)
has a density wrt the Lebesgue measure bounded uniformly by
C>0
.
Then, we have for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\delta+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|>\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right).

(135)

Proof.

Let
\delta>0
. On the one hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}-|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}-\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|>\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)+C\delta+\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|>\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right),

because, by assumption,
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(q_{k}\leq W(X_{k})\leq q_{k}+\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)\leq C\delta
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}+|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}+\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|>\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W(X_{k})>q_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right)-C\delta-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(|W_{k}(X_{k})-W(X_{k})|>\delta\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right).

This establishes (
135
).
∎

C.2
General lemmas

Lemma C.6
.

For
\beta\in(1/2,1)
, we have as
n
tends to infinity

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n-\Delta_{n}}^{n}j^{-\beta}

\displaystyle\sim\Delta_{n}n^{-\beta}\>;

(136)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n-\Delta_{n}}^{n}j^{-2\beta}

\displaystyle\sim\Delta_{n}n^{-2\beta}\>,

(137)

provided that
n^{\beta}\ll\Delta_{n}\ll n
(and
\Delta_{n}
is an integer).
Note that
\Delta_{n}n^{-\beta}
tends to infinity in (
136
) and
\Delta_{n}n^{-2\beta}
tends to to
0
in (
137
).
In addition, for any
c\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\lfloor nc\rfloor}^{n}j^{-\beta}

\displaystyle\sim(1-\beta)^{-1}(1-c^{1-\beta})n^{1-\beta}\>;

(138)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\lfloor nc\rfloor}^{n}j^{-2\beta}

\displaystyle\sim(2\beta-1)^{-1}(c^{1-2\beta}-1)n^{1-2\beta}\>,

(139)

Proof.

Let
\gamma>0
with
\gamma\neq 1
. Let
1<m\leq M
two integers. We have classically for all
j\in[m,M]
,
\int_{j}^{j+1}u^{-\gamma}\operatorname{d}\!u\leq j^{-\gamma}\leq\int_{j-1}^{j}u^{-\gamma}\operatorname{d}\!u
since
\gamma>0
. By summing this inequality we get:

\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((M+1)^{1-\gamma}-m^{1-\gamma}}}\right)\leq\sum_{j=m}^{M}j^{-\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M^{1-\gamma}-(m-1)^{1-\gamma}}}\right).

(140)

Let us first prove (
138
) and (
139
). We use (
140
) with
M=n
and
m=\lfloor{nc\rfloor}
. We have for the left-hand-side and right-hand-side of (
140
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((n+1)^{1-\gamma}-\lfloor{nc\rfloor}^{1-\gamma}}}\right)=\frac{n^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((1+n^{-1})^{1-\gamma}-(\lfloor{nc\rfloor}n^{-1})^{1-\gamma}}}\right)}\sim\frac{n^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-c}}\right)^{1-\gamma};

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n^{1-\gamma}-\lfloor{(n-1)c\rfloor}^{1-\gamma}}}\right)=\frac{n^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-(\lfloor{(n-1)c\rfloor}n^{-1})^{1-\gamma}}}\right)}\sim\frac{n^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-c}}\right)^{1-\gamma},

which, by using (
140
) gives (
138
) and (
139
) since for
\beta\in(1/2,1)
,
2\beta>1
.

Let us prove (
136
) and (
137
). We now take
M=n
and
m=n-\Delta_{n}
. We study the right hand side of (
140
). We have
(1-\gamma)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n^{1-\gamma}-(n-\Delta_{n}-1)^{1-\gamma}}}\right)={n^{1-\gamma}}(1-\gamma)^{-1}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-(1-\Delta_{n}n^{-1}-n^{-1})^{1-\gamma}}}\right)}
, and since
\Delta_{n}n^{-1}\rightarrow 0
, one can use the Taylor expansion of
x\mapsto(1+x)^{1-\gamma}
near
0
to obtain the following expansion:

\displaystyle(1-\gamma)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(n^{1-\gamma}-(n-\Delta_{n}-1)^{1-\gamma}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((1-\gamma)(\Delta_{n}n^{-1}+n^{-1})+o(\Delta_{n}n^{-1}+n^{-1}))}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{n}n^{-\gamma}+n^{-\gamma}+o(\Delta_{n}n^{-\gamma}+n^{-\gamma}),

and since
n^{-\gamma}=o(\Delta_{n}n^{-\gamma})
(because
\Delta_{n}\rightarrow+\infty
), we have that the right-hand-side of (
140
) is equivalent, when
n
tends to infinity, to
\Delta_{n}n^{-\gamma}
. With the same computation, we have that the left hand side is also equivalent to
\Delta_{n}n^{-\gamma}
, which concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma C.7
.

Let
U
be any random variable with values in
[A_{1},A_{2}]
, for
0\leq A_{1}<A_{2}\leq 1
. Consider the function
\Psi:u\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathbb{E}(U\>|\>U<u)
, with the convention
\Psi(u)=A_{1}
when
\mathbb{P}(U<u)=0
. Then the following holds:

(i)

\Psi
is nondecreasing on
[0,1]
with
\Psi(0)=A_{1}
and
\Psi(1)=\mathbb{E}(U)
;

(ii)

if
U
has a continuous density
g
on
[A_{1},A_{2}]
, which is positive on
(A_{1},A_{2})
, then
\Psi
is also increasing on
[A_{1},A_{2}]
and continuous on
[0,1]
. In addition,
\Psi
is differentiable on
(A_{1},A_{2})
with
\Psi^{\prime}(u)=\frac{ug(u)}{\mathbb{P}(U<u)}
,
u\in(A_{1},A_{2})
, and
\sup_{u\in[u_{0},v_{0}]}\Psi^{\prime}(u)<+\infty
,
\inf_{u\in[u_{0},v_{0}]}\Psi^{\prime}(u)>0
for any
A_{1}<u_{0}<v_{0}<A_{2}
.

Proof.

Let us first check point (i).
Take
A_{1}\leq u<u^{\prime}\leq A_{2}
and show that
\Psi(u)\leq\Psi(u^{\prime})
. If
\mathbb{P}(U<u)=0
, then
\Psi(u)=0
and the inequality is trivial. Now suppose
\mathbb{P}(U<u)>0
and let
\gamma=\mathbb{P}(U<u\>|\>U<u^{\prime})=\frac{\mathbb{P}(U<u)}{\mathbb{P}(U<u^{\prime})}\in[0,1]
. We have

\displaystyle\Psi(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(U\>|\>U<u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(U{\mathbf{1}\{U<u\}}\>|\>U<u^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}(U{\mathbf{1}\{u\leq U<u^{\prime}\}}\>|\>U<u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\gamma\mathbb{E}(U\>|\>U<u)+(1-\gamma)\mathbb{E}(U\>|\>u\leq U<u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq\gamma\Psi(u)+(1-\gamma)u\geq\Psi(u),

because
\Psi(u)\leq u
. This gives the first statement.

Let us now check (ii).
Since in that case
\Psi(u)=\frac{\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[U{\mathbf{1}\{U<u\}}}}\right]}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u}}\right)}
for
u\in(A_{1},1]
, it is clear that
\Psi
is continuous on
(A_{1},1]
. In addition,
\Psi(u)=A_{1}
for
u<A_{1}
and
A_{1}\leq\Psi(A_{1}+\epsilon)\leq A_{1}+\epsilon
for
\epsilon>0
small enough. Hence,
\Psi
is also continuous in
A_{1}
and thus on
[0,1]
.

Consider
A_{1}<u_{0}<v_{0}<A_{2}
and write for each
u\in[u_{0},v_{0}]
and
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\Psi(u+\varepsilon)-\Psi(u-\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[U\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{U<u+\varepsilon\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u+\varepsilon}}\right)}-\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{U<u-\varepsilon\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u-\varepsilon}}\right)}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u}}\right)}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[U{\mathbf{1}\{u-\varepsilon\leq U<u+\varepsilon\}}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u}}\right)}\int_{u-\varepsilon}^{u+\varepsilon}xg(x)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{0}\varepsilon}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u_{0}}}\right)},

where
g
denotes the density of
U
and
C_{0}=\max_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2}]}g(x)
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle\Psi(u+\varepsilon)-\Psi(u-\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[U\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{U<u+\varepsilon\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u+\varepsilon}}\right)}-\frac{{\mathbf{1}\{U<u-\varepsilon\}}}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u-\varepsilon}}\right)}}}\right)}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U<u}}\right)}-C_{0}\varepsilon}}\right)\int_{u-\varepsilon}^{u+\varepsilon}xg(x)dx

\displaystyle\geq(c_{0}u_{0}/2)\varepsilon,

where
c_{0}=\min_{x\in[(A_{1}+u_{0})/2,(A_{2}+v_{0})/2]}g(x)
.
Combining this entails that for any
u\in(A_{1},A_{2})
,

\frac{ug(u)}{\mathbb{P}(U<u)}\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon}\frac{\Psi(u+\varepsilon)-\Psi(u-\varepsilon)}{2\varepsilon}\leq\limsup_{\varepsilon}\frac{\Psi(u+\varepsilon)-\Psi(u-\varepsilon)}{2\varepsilon}\leq\frac{ug(u)}{\mathbb{P}(U<u)},

hence
\Psi
is differentiable on
(A_{1},A_{2})
,
\Psi^{\prime}(u)=\frac{ug(u)}{\mathbb{P}(U<u)}
,
u\in(A_{1},A_{2})
,
\sup_{u\in[u_{0},v_{0}]}\Psi^{\prime}(u)<+\infty
and
\inf_{u\in[u_{0},v_{0}]}\Psi^{\prime}(u)>0
. This gives in particular that
\Psi
is increasing on
(A_{1},A_{2})
and thus also on
[A_{1},A_{2}]
(because
\Psi
is nondecreasing on
[A_{1},A_{2}]
by (i)).
∎

Lemma C.8
.

Let
f
being a bounded density (wrt the Lebesgue measure) such that
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|xf(x)}}\right\rvert<\infty
.
Then the corresponding upper-tail function
\bar{F}(x)=\int_{x}^{\infty}f(t)dt
is such that for all
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}>0
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
h\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x+\frac{h}{\sigma_{2}}}}\right)-\bar{F}(x)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}{\sigma_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|h}}\right\rvert+2(1\vee\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|xf(x)}}\right\rvert)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sigma_{1}/\sigma_{2}-1}}\right\rvert.

(141)

Proof.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
h\in\mathbb{R}
. We have:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x+\frac{h}{\sigma_{2}}}}\right)-\bar{F}(x)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x+\frac{h}{\sigma_{2}}}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x}}\right)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[x+\frac{h}{\sigma_{1}}}}\right]}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x}}\right)}}\right\rvert+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right\rvert.

By the mean value inequality, we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[x+\frac{h}{\sigma_{1}}}}\right]}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}x}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lVert f}}\right\rVert_{\infty}}{\sigma_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|h}}\right\rvert.

Let us bound the second term. Denote
\gamma=\sigma_{1}/\sigma_{2}>0
for short. We first assume that
\gamma\geq 1/2
. By the mean value inequality with the function
u\in[0,\infty)\mapsto\bar{F}(ux)
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma x}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq\sup_{u\in[\gamma\wedge 1,\gamma\vee 1]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|x}}\right\rvert f(ux)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma-1}}\right\rvert\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|x}}\right\rvert f(x)}}\right)\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma-1}}\right\rvert}{\gamma\wedge 1}\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|x}}\right\rvert f(x)}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma-1}}\right\rvert.

In addition, for
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
, we have
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma x}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq 1\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma-1}}\right\rvert
. This gives, for all
\gamma>0
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\gamma x}}\right)-\bar{F}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right\rvert\leq 2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1\vee\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|x}}\right\rvert f(x)}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\gamma-1}}\right\rvert,

and finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.9

(Bennet inequality for Binomial random variable)
.

Let
p\in[0,1]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\{0\}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{B}(n,p)-np\geq\kappa\sqrt{n\log(n)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{B}(n,p)-np\geq-\kappa\sqrt{n\log(n)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1},

with
\kappa:=(9\mathrm{e})^{-1}(18\mathrm{e}+3\sqrt{\mathrm{e}}+1)
a universal constant.

Lemma C.10

(
Bercu et al., 2015
)
.

Let
(M_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a square integrable martingale adapted to a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
M_{0}=0
and consider
\langle M\rangle_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Delta M_{k}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}}}\right]
where
\Delta M_{n}=M_{n}-M_{n-1}
.
Let
n\geq 1
and assume that for
k\in[n]
, we have
A_{k}\leq\Delta M_{k}\leq B_{k}
almost surely, where
A_{k}
and
B_{k}
are bounded
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
-measurable random variables.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(B_{k}-A_{k})^{2}
.
Then for
x,y>0
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{n}\geq x,2\langle M\rangle_{n}+\mathcal{D}_{n}\leq y}}\right)\leq e^{-3x^{2}/y}.

Appendix D
Algorithms

Input :
Targeted level
\alpha\in(0,1)
, decreasing step size
(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 1}
, initial threshold
q_{1}
, initial training data
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
, classification algorithm
\mathcal{A}
taking as input labeled observations and returning estimated probabilities of being in each class.

J\leftarrow 0
;

1

\mathcal{D}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{0}
;

2

for

t\geq 1

do

3

\pi_{t}\leftarrow\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{D}}}}\right)
, with
\pi_{t}(x,y)
being an estimation of
\mathbb{P}(Y_{t}=y\>|\>X_{t}=x)
;

4

Observe
X_{t}
;

5

\widehat{y}_{t}\leftarrow\operatorname{argmax}_{y\in[K]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\pi_{t}(X_{t},y)}}\right\}
(point prediction);

6

\mathcal{C}_{t}=\{\widehat{y}_{t}\}
(prediction set);

7

if

\pi_{t}(X_{t},\widehat{y}_{t})\geq 1-q_{t}

then

8

S_{t}\leftarrow 1
(select
X_{t}
);

9

J\leftarrow J+1
;

10

Observe
Y_{t}
;

11

if

q_{t}\geq 0

and

Y_{t}=\widehat{y}_{t}

then

12

q_{t}\leftarrow q_{t}-\gamma_{J}\alpha
;

13

end if

14

else

15

q_{t}\leftarrow q_{t}+\gamma_{J}(1-\alpha)
;

16

end if

17

end if

18

else

19

S_{t}\leftarrow 0
(do not select
X_{t}
);

20

Observe
Y_{t}
;

21

end if

22

\mathcal{D}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\cup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(X_{t},Y_{t}}}\right)}}\right\}
;

23

end for

24

Output :
At each time
t
, selection indicator
S_{t}
and point prediction
\mathcal{C}_{t}=\{\widehat{y}_{t}\}
for the selected times.

Algorithm 2

Online selective classification

Input :
Targeted level
\alpha\in(0,1)
, nominal value
y_{0}
, decreasing step size
(\gamma_{t})_{t\geq 1}
, initial threshold
q_{1}
, initial training data
\mathcal{D}_{0}
,
prediction algorithm
\mathcal{A}
taking as input labeled observations and returning an estimator of
\mu(x)=\mathbb{E}(Y_{t}\mid X_{t}=x)
.

J\leftarrow 0
;

1

\mathcal{D}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{0}
;

2

for

t\geq 1

do

3

\hat{\mu}_{t}(\cdot)\leftarrow\mathcal{A}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\mathcal{D}}}}\right)
(estimation at time
t
);

4

Observe
X_{t}
;

5

if

\hat{\mu}_{t}(x)+\Phi^{-1}(1-q)>y_{0}

then

6

S_{t}\leftarrow 1
(select
X_{t}
);

7

J\leftarrow J+1
;

8

\mathcal{C}_{t}\leftarrow[\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})+\Phi^{-1}(1-q),+\infty)
(prediction region);

9

Observe
Y_{t}
;

10

if

q\geq 0

and

\hat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t})+\Phi^{-1}(1-q)\leq Y_{t}

then

11

q\leftarrow q-\gamma_{J}\alpha
;

12

13

else

;

15

end if

16

end if

17

else

18

S_{t}\leftarrow 0
(do not select
X_{t}
);

19

Observe
Y_{t}
;
end if

20

\mathcal{D}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\cup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(X_{t},Y_{t}}}\right)}}\right\}
;

21

end for

22

Output :
At each time
t
, selection indicator
S_{t}
and one-sided prediction set
\mathcal{C}_{t}
with a lower bound larger than
y_{0}
for the selected times.
